# Geiger research: verified evidence log

Generated from the geiger-research workflow journal on 2026-11-07. Every claim was checked by a separate fact-checker who re-opened the cited sources. Kept claims are `confirmed` or `corrected` (the corrected wording is shown). Refuted, unverifiable and unverified claims are listed under each area as dropped.

## Is "metric-driven agentic coding" an established term, and what comes up for it?

- **term-1** (corrected) [inference]: The exact phrase "metric-driven agentic coding" is not an established term. Repeated standard and extended web searches on 2026-11-07, a domain-limited extended search, and HN Algolia found no page that uses the exact phrase. The closest match is one HN comment that says "metric driven agentic refactors" (term-2), so it is a near-match, not a verbatim hit. No cited page defines a related name such as "metric-driven agentic" or "metrics-driven agentic". The conclusion rests only on missing search results; closed social media is not covered.
  - [HN comment by aleyan on 'Uncle Bob: It's Over'](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47999904) (2026-05-03): "I found metric driven agentic refactors just push complexity to outside the scope of the metric."
  - Fact-check: I reran the searches. Standard and extended web searches for "metric-driven agentic coding", the extended search limited to github.com, HN, x.com, linkedin.com, medium.com and substack.com, and an extended search for "metric-driven agentic" OR "metrics-driven agentic" did not surface the phrase. I also downloaded 10 top result pages (CodeRabbit, Warp, Larridin, Port, CallSphere, CodeScene, dekobon agent-feedback, understandingdata, DEV dmitryame, arXiv 2609.04681), the agentic_coding_lab README and the Ronacher gist, and grepped each for 'metrics?[- ]driven agentic': 0 hits. HN Algolia (advancedSyntax) gave "metric-driven agentic coding" 0 hits and "metric-driven agentic" / "metric driven agentic" 1 hit, item 47999904, which reads "I found metric driven agentic refactors just push complexity to outside the scope of the metric." The inference holds. Only the wording "only verbatim hit" overstates: the comment matches part of the phrase, not all of it.
- **term-2** (corrected) [term-usage]: The phrase appears only as "metric driven agentic refactors", in a PS to an HN comment by aleyan (2026-05-03T18:29:54Z) on the story "Uncle Bob: It's Over". Most of the comment is about agent-written tests being perfunctory and over-mocked. The PS says, in reaction to Uncle Bob, that metric-driven agentic refactors push complexity outside what the metric measures, and that the commenter must actively guide agents so refactors improve the code without increasing the codebase's entropy. The comment does not name the metrics (not CRAP or any Uncle Bob tool). It does not show that Uncle Bob advocated refactoring against metrics; it only says his post was about agents writing tests and refactoring code.
  - [HN comment on 'Uncle Bob: It's Over' (story 47998601)](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47999904) (2026-05-03): "I am finding I need to actively guide the agents for the refactors to actually improve things without increasing the entropy of the codebase."
  - Fact-check: I opened https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47999904. Exact text: "PS. Reacting to Uncle Bob, I found metric driven agentic refactors just push complexity to outside the scope of the metric." and "I need to actively guide the agents for the refactors to actually improve things without increasing the entropy of the codebase." The comment also says "the subject matter, which is about having agents write tests and refactor code." HN Algolia API: created_at 2026-05-03T18:29:54Z, author aleyan, parent story 47998601 "Uncle Bob: It's Over" (a reddit link). The claim's "Uncle Bob's push for agents to refactor against metrics" and "reduce codebase entropy" overstate the source, which says avoid increasing entropy. The relevance note's "CRAP-style metrics from Uncle Bob's tools" is not in the comment. I did not open the linked X post.
- **term-3** [term-usage]: Metrics-Driven Development (MDD) is an older DevOps term. InfoQ published Mantas Klasavičius's article on 2012-11-30, based on his DevOpsDays Rome talk. In his usage, metrics are runtime, usage and revenue telemetry that drive development, not static code-quality measures.
  - [Metrics Driven Development (InfoQ)](https://www.infoq.com/articles/metrics-driven-development/) (2012-11-30): "I would define MDD as a practice where metrics are used to drive the entire application development. In a company which uses MDD, everything from performance and usage patterns to revenue is measured."
- **term-4** [term-usage]: In 2016, Kevin Sookocheff still called Metrics-Driven Development an emerging term derived from CI/CD, DevOps and agile. He defined it by real-time metrics, which confirms the runtime-telemetry meaning. Early HN posts with the term date from 2012 to 2015 (7digital StatsD/Graphite, Librato, Benjamin Wootton).
  - [Metrics-Driven Development (Kevin Sookocheff)](https://sookocheff.com/post/mdd/mdd/) (2016-08-09): "Metrics-Driven Development (MDD) The use of real-time metrics to drive rapid, precise, and granular software iterations."
- **term-5** [term-usage]: "Architectural fitness function" is an established term defined in Building Evolutionary Architectures (Ford, Parsons, Kua; O'Reilly 2017; 2nd ed. 2022 adds Sadalage). Thoughtworks Technology Radar listed it at Trial in Nov 2017 and May 2018.
  - [Architectural fitness function (Thoughtworks Technology Radar)](https://www.thoughtworks.com/radar/techniques/architectural-fitness-function) (2017-11-30): "An architectural fitness function, as defined in Building Evolutionary Architectures, provides an objective integrity assessment of some architectural characteristics."
- **term-6** [term-usage]: Birgitta Böckeler (Thoughtworks, on martinfowler.com, 2026-04-02) frames harness engineering for coding agents as guides (feedforward) and sensors (feedback). She names an "architecture fitness harness" that is "basically" fitness functions. Her examples include ArchUnit module-boundary tests run in a coding-agent hook, and computational sensors for duplication and complexity.
  - [Harness engineering for coding agent users (Böckeler)](https://martinfowler.com/articles/harness-engineering.html) (2026-04-02): "Computational sensors catch the structural stuff reliably: duplicate code, cyclomatic complexity, missing test coverage, architectural drift, style violations."
  - [Harness engineering for coding agent users (Böckeler)](https://martinfowler.com/articles/harness-engineering.html) (2026-04-02): "This groups guides and sensors that define and check the architecture characteristics of the application. Basically: Fitness Functions ."
- **term-7** [fact]: Böckeler's memo from 2026-02-17 summarizes OpenAI's harness-engineering report (Ryan Lopopolo, Feb 2026). In it, architectural constraints were enforced by deterministic custom linters and structural tests, and periodic agents did "garbage collection" against decay. She criticizes the report for lacking verification of behaviour and notes OpenAI's commercial interest. The OpenAI page itself returned 403 and was not read.
  - [Harness Engineering - first thoughts (Böckeler)](https://www.martinfowler.com/articles/exploring-gen-ai/harness-engineering-memo.html) (2026-02-17): "Monitored not only by the LLM-based agents, but also deterministic custom linters and structural tests."
- **term-8** [term-usage]: InfoQ (2026-08-17; Mahato, Sieczkowski, Kuppusamy) introduced "agentic fitness functions": LLM-judged architecture checks with rubrics. The article states that deterministic fitness functions should stay the primary enforcement for measurable invariants such as dependency direction.
  - [Agentic Fitness Functions: Extending Evolutionary Architecture Beyond Deterministic Rules (InfoQ)](https://www.infoq.com/articles/agentic-fitness-functions-evolutionary-architecture/) (2026-08-17): "Deterministic fitness functions should remain the primary enforcement mechanism for measurable invariants such as dependency direction, contract shape, latency budgets, security posture, and policy checks."
- **term-9** [fact]: Neal Ford, co-author of Building Evolutionary Architectures, offers a 2026 workshop on deriving deterministic guardrails for human and AI agents from architecture rules. The page was created on 2026-05-29. Fitness functions are thus being explicitly repositioned for coding agents by their originator.
  - [Agents and Architecture training (Neal Ford)](https://nealford.com/training/agentsandarch.html) (2026-05-29): "how to define architecture rules embedded within specifications and how to use the same rules to generate deterministic guardrails. Even with the best models, developers should trust but verify"
- **term-10** [term-usage]: Error budgets come from Google's SRE book (chapter "Embracing Risk"). The budget is the gap between the SLO and measured reliability. Releases continue while budget remains and halt when it is spent.
  - [Site Reliability Engineering, Ch. 3: Embracing Risk](https://sre.google/sre-book/embracing-risk/) (2016): "If SLO violations occur frequently enough to expend the error budget, releases are temporarily halted while additional resources are invested in system testing and development"
- **term-11** (corrected) [term-usage]: "Tech debt budget" is used informally for the share of engineering capacity reserved for debt work (for example a 20% tech debt budget, tianpan.co forum, 2026-03). No authoritative percentage exists (OneUptime, 2026-08-12, which itself does not use the term "debt budget"). One search found no use of the term for an allowed loss of a quality score. Separately, SRE sources link error budgets to when debt gets paid down.
  - [Reserve Sprint Capacity for Improvement and Technical Debt (OneUptime, Nawaz Dhandala)](https://oneuptime.com/blog/post/2026-08-12-reserve-sprint-capacity-improvement-technical-debt/view) (2026-08-12): "There is no authoritative Scrum percentage for improvement work or technical debt."
  - Fact-check: Opened the OneUptime post (Nawaz Dhandala, 2026-08-12). It contains the quote exactly: "There is no authoritative Scrum percentage for improvement work or technical debt." It never uses "technical debt budget" or "debt budget" (the closest term is "Debt bucket"), so it supports the no-authoritative-percentage part but not the usage claim. A separate source supports the usage: https://tianpan.co/forum/t/the-20-tech-debt-budget-engineering-best-practice-or-investor-red-flag/1540 ("The 20% Tech Debt Budget", cto_michelle, 2026-03-13) quotes "dedicate 20-30% of your engineering capacity to managing technical debt." A search found SRE writing that ties error budgets to debt paydown (Google CRE blog), but no use of the term for an allowed loss of a quality score. That absence comes from a single search and is not proof. The 403'd Medium post could not be checked.
- **term-12** [term-usage]: "Eval-driven development" is established in LLM application engineering, and OpenAI's evaluation best-practices guide names it. It means writing evals of model or agent behaviour early. It does not mean scoring the code an agent writes.
  - [Evaluation best practices (OpenAI API docs)](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/evaluation-best-practices) (undated; retrieved 2026-11-07): "Adopt eval-driven development: Evaluate early and often. Write scoped tests at every stage."
- **term-13** [term-usage]: "Quality gates" is the mainstream term for constraints on agent changes. Addy Osmani (2026-08-08) uses it for tests, mutation testing, and complexity and line-length metrics, together with "back-pressure". He also states that quality is not a single metric.
  - [Agentic Code Quality (Addy Osmani)](https://addyosmani.com/blog/agentic-code-quality/) (2026-08-08): "We call these constraints quality gates, and they take many forms."
  - [Agentic Code Quality (Addy Osmani)](https://addyosmani.com/blog/agentic-code-quality/) (2026-08-08): "Software quality isn’t a single metric. Think of it as a collection of signals of varying importance to you and your team."
- **term-14** [tool-capability]: SonarQube Cloud ships a built-in "Sonar way for agentic AI" quality gate, which replaces the legacy "Sonar way for AI code" gate. Its conditions apply to new code only, for example coverage on new code ≥80% and duplication on new code ≤3%. This is existing tooling for scoring only the changed area.
  - [Quality gate for agentic AI (SonarQube Cloud docs)](https://docs.sonarsource.com/sonarqube-cloud/standards/ai-code-assurance/quality-gate-for-agentic-ai) (undated; retrieved 2026-11-07): "The Sonar way for agentic AI quality gate is a built-in quality gate optimized for agent centric development lifecycles where AI agents are generating or modifying code."
- **term-15** [empirical-result]: CodeScene and loveholidays reported at TechDebt 2026 (industry track, 2026-04-12) that they put CodeScene's CodeHealth metric into the Claude Code loop as a guardrail. Agents iterated on low-CodeHealth additions until they reached a threshold of 10, and CodeHealth rose as output grew. The report is co-authored by the vendor and was not peer-reviewed as research.
  - [Quality Feedback in the Agentic Loop: Using CodeHealth Guardrails to Agentic Code Improvement (Borg, Caborn)](https://conf.researchr.org/details/TechDebt-2026/TechDebt-2026-industry-track/1/Quality-Feedback-in-the-Agentic-Loop-Using-CodeHealth-Guardrails-to-Agentic-Code-Imp) (2026-04-12): "The agents iterated on any low-CodeHealth code additions until they reached a predefined threshold, intentionally set at a high value (a perfect 10)."
- **term-16** [term-usage]: Practitioners describe ratchets, in which quality may only move in one direction, as a guardrail for coding agents. Gary Pendergast (2026-04-13) uses a coverage ratchet and recommends binary pass/fail signals.
  - [Guardrails for Agentic Development (Gary Pendergast)](https://pento.net/?p=5839) (2026-04-13): "Ratcheting. Quality should only move in one direction. Coding agents will always skip something they decide is too hard; you need to stop that from happening."
- **term-17** [empirical-result]: Several sources warn that metrics shown to agents get gamed. The big-code-analysis (bca) docs say bare metric feedback leads to score-lowering helper extraction without better readability. The agentic_coding_lab README says that once a prompt names a metric, the metric becomes a compliance signal rather than an independent observation.
  - [Feeding metrics to an agentic coding tool (big-code-analysis docs)](https://dekobon.github.io/big-code-analysis/recipes/agent-feedback.html) (undated; references Claude Code 2.1.139): "Bare metric feedback can encourage changes that lower scores without improving readability, such as extracting helpers with no useful responsibility."
  - [agentic_coding_lab README (marcoemrich)](https://github.com/marcoemrich/agentic_coding_lab/blob/main/README.md) (2026 (README references runs through 2026-10)): "runs of that workflow no longer produce independent observations of that metric — they produce compliance signals."
- **term-18** [fact]: A long evidence report hosted by Peter Roelants (v1.26, evidence through 2026-08-22, researched by OpenAI Codex under his direction) argues against a universal code-quality score and against using letter grades as an agent reward. It recommends ratchets on changed code. It never uses "metric-driven" or "fitness function" (grep of the raw gist).
  - [Code quality in agentic software engineering, v1.26 (Peter Roelants gist)](https://gist.github.com/peterroelants/69029d4100a99e22dbb7df60a14c286b) (2026-08-22): "Five problems make a universal code-quality score misleading."
  - [Code quality in agentic software engineering, v1.26 (Peter Roelants gist)](https://gist.github.com/peterroelants/69029d4100a99e22dbb7df60a14c286b) (2026-08-22): "use the dashboard for triage, not its letter grade as the reward function"
- **term-19** [fact]: In ML research, Karpathy's autoresearch (March 2026) popularized an agent loop driven by a single metric: an agent modifies code, runs a fixed 5-minute training run, checks val_bpb, and keeps or discards the change. When people say "metric-driven agent loop", this is a likely association, and it means optimizing an outcome metric rather than preserving code quality.
  - [karpathy/autoresearch README](https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch) (2026-03): "It modifies the code, trains for 5 minutes, checks if the result improved, keeps or discards, and repeats."
- **term-20** (corrected) [inference]: Recognized vocabulary for Geiger: "fitness functions" / "architecture rules" and "quality gates" (pass/fail) for hard gates; "ratchets" on changed or new code and "budgets" for soft score deltas, following Roelants' gate/ratchet-and-budget split ("quality budget" by analogy with SRE error budgets needs an explicit definition because "debt budget" usually means capacity share); "harness sensors" (Böckeler) or "back-pressure" (Osmani) for feedback to agents. "Metric-driven agentic coding" is not an established term; searches found no prior use of the exact phrase, so it would need defining.
  - [Harness engineering for coding agent users (Böckeler)](https://martinfowler.com/articles/harness-engineering.html) (2026-04-02): "The term harness has emerged as a shorthand to mean everything in an AI agent except the model itself - Agent = Model + Harness ."
  - Fact-check: Opened the Fowler/Böckeler harness-engineering page (2 April 2026). It defines the harness ("Agent = Model + Harness"), uses "sensors" throughout, and links "Fitness Functions". It does not use back-pressure, quality gate, ratchet or budget. "Back-pressure" comes from Osmani. Two parts of the inference do not follow. First, "quality gates" is pass/fail vocabulary in the cited sources: Osmani calls constraints quality gates, Pendergast says "Pass or fail", and Sonar gates block. Assigning the term to soft score deltas is a mismatch. Second, Roelants' gist puts it this way: "Gates fit exact or high-confidence rules" and "Ratchets and budgets fit noisy structural signals". It also says "Exact architecture violations can be gates" (https://gist.github.com/peterroelants/69029d4100a99e22dbb7df60a14c286b, lines 420 and 426-427). Also unsupported: "its only prior use is critical". Exact-phrase searches for "metric-driven agentic coding" found no established use, and Roelants does not use "metric-driven". No critical prior use was shown. The "quality budget" analogy is reasonable, but per term-11 the common meaning of "debt budget" is capacity, so Geiger must define the term.

### Open questions

- Who first proposed the 'technical debt budget' as an SRE error-budget analogue? The Medium post by Rodrigo Estrada returned 403 and was not read.
- Uncle Bob's X post (status 2046206145597972849, April 2026) that prompted the HN thread was not read. Does he use 'metric-driven' or similar wording for agents refactoring against crap/dry/mutation metrics?
- OpenAI's 'Harness engineering' post (Feb 2026) returned 403, so its exact wording on architecture linters, quality grades and garbage collection is known only through Böckeler's summary.
- Is there any published empirical study, beyond vendor-co-authored ones, showing that per-PR quality-score budgets for agents reduce long-term maintainability loss, or that they induce gaming?
- Does SonarQube or CodeScene support a per-PR score-delta budget (for example ≤0.2 points) rather than absolute thresholds on new code?

## Products and open-source tools that score or gate code changes, especially changes by AI coding agents

- **land-1** [tool-capability]: CodeScene's MCP server returns a per-file Code Health score from 10.0 (best) to 1.0 (worst). It has two change-scoped gate tools. `pre_commit_code_health_safeguard` reviews staged files. `analyze_change_set` reviews all files that differ from a base ref and returns pass/fail quality gates plus a per-file verdict: improved, degraded or stable.
  - [CodeScene MCP Server - Tools (docs/tools.md)](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/codescene-oss/codescene-mcp-server/main/docs/tools.md) (fetched 2026-11-07): "Run a branch-level Code Health review for all files that differ between the current HEAD and a base ref."
  - [CodeScene MCP Server - Tools (docs/tools.md)](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/codescene-oss/codescene-mcp-server/main/docs/tools.md) (fetched 2026-11-07): "Returns one numeric score from 10.0 (optimal) to 1.0 (worst)."
- **land-2** [fact]: CodeScene fully supports Rust: Code Health metrics, the virtual code reviewer and Rust-specific rules for inline test modules. Its codebase-level 'Average Code Health' weights each file by its lines of code, so one small degraded file barely moves the average.
  - [CodeScene - Supported Programming Languages](https://codescene.io/docs/usage/language-support.html) (fetched 2026-11-07): "Full language support means that CodeScene calculates Code Health metrics, enables the virtual code reviewer,"
  - [CodeScene - Code Health](https://codescene.io/docs/guides/technical/code-health.html) (fetched 2026-11-07): "The code health scores are aggregated by a weighted average. The weight is the number of lines of code (LoC) in each file."
- **land-3** (corrected) [market]: The CodeScene MCP server is not fully open source. Its LICENSE mixes Apache-2.0 open-source components with closed-source components. Its full feature set (hotspots, technical-debt goals, code ownership, usage overview) needs a CodeScene subscription. Local Code Health analysis, including the score, review, pre-commit safeguard and analyze_change_set tools, is also available under a 'standalone' license without a CodeScene subscription. ACE auto-refactoring needs an additional license. Code Health analysis runs locally. Project-insight data is fetched over REST from the user's CodeScene account.
  - [codescene-mcp-server LICENSE](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/codescene-oss/codescene-mcp-server/main/LICENSE) (fetched 2026-11-07): "This Model Context Protocol Server ("MCP Server") includes both open-source and closed-source software components."
  - [CodeScene MCP Server docs](https://codescene.io/docs/developer-tools/mcp/codescene-mcp-server.html) (fetched 2026-11-07): "The CodeScene MCP Server runs fully locally."
  - Fact-check: The LICENSE quote is present (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/codescene-oss/codescene-mcp-server/main/LICENSE). The docs page https://codescene.io/docs/developer-tools/mcp/codescene-mcp-server.html lists 'An active CodeScene subscription (Cloud or on-prem)' as a prerequisite and says "ACE requires an additional license." It also says "The CodeScene MCP Server runs fully locally." The repo README contradicts the blanket subscription requirement (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/codescene-oss/codescene-mcp-server/main/README.md): "For local Code Health analysis without a CodeScene subscription, you can use the standalone CodeScene Code Health MCP". It also says: "The full feature set — including hotspots, technical debt goals, and code ownership — requires a CodeScene subscription." docs/tools.md marks the analysis and gate tools 'All Users' (standalone or API-connected). The docs page adds: "Analysis results (e.g. hotspots and technical debt goals) are fetched via REST from your own CodeScene account". The relevance note says custom domain rules are impossible. That is an inference, consistent with the rules-config tools, which only enable or disable rules and set thresholds and weights.
